## 2011 United States National Championship

![](https://i.ormos.uk/ff/7d/ff7d190aa5e1e3152fd582eae7014592bd548beda716ae760787833a00725b86.jpg)

Leading up to U.S. Nationals, one question was on everyone's mind: what will the changes to Standard do to the grievous specter of CawBlade. Well, after a weekends worth of battling, I think we have an answer now. Four of the top eleven Standard decks from U.S. Nationals were the mighty UW beast, a number that is considerably lower than almost certainly would have been seen before the changes. Six of them made Top 8, but their pilots included some of the most high profile players in the game today, like David Ochoa, Owen Turtenwald, and Luis Scott-Vargas. There was a significant resurgence in the other decks that had been forced to hide in CawBlade's shadow, resulting in a wonderfully balanced and varied field.

After seventeen rounds of fighting through Standard and *M12* Draft, eight players were left alone to vie for the three spots on the U.S. National team. The first round saw some big names fall. Owen Turtenwald and Luis Scott-Vargas both fell to their respective opponents. This marked the third U.S. Nationals Top 8 for LSV, and would have been his third team had he made it. Joining them on the sidelines to watch how things played out were Noah Koessel and James McLeod. The semifinals whittled it down once more, sending Haibing Hu and Brandon Nelson to battle things out for the third spot on the U.S. National Team roster.

That just left David Ochoa, last year's alternate, and Ali Aintrazi to fight for the right to be called champion. Both men were guaranteed spots on this year's team, but only one got to lift the champion's trophy. The opportunity to represent the United States at Worlds as quite a far cry from Ali Aintrazi's humble beginnings on the tcgplayer.com circuit. He's carved out quite a name for himself in the past years, and has proven that he deserves to be here and will represent the U.S. well, no matter the outcome. This marks the second National team that David Ochoa has made in a row, the odds of which are astounding. He was just the alternate last year, but this year he was not only guaranteed a spot, but fighting for the whole championship.

After a tense battle, Aintrazi's UB Control deck won out, taking four games to claim victory, becoming the 2011 U.S. National Champion! The third-place playoff was the last thing needed to decide the full roster for the American team. It took all five games, but Haibing Hu was able to edge out Brandon Nelson and claim the final spot on the roster. After all the excitement, with everything in the books, congratulations to Ali Aintrazi, David Ochoa, and Haibing Hu: your U.S. National team!

![](https://i.ormos.uk/d5/fc/d5fc96cca1131493da7f846922e7d0ac0b88e793357d7b2fcddad21d71cc43cd.jpg)

## Top 8 Player profiles

![](https://i.ormos.uk/b7/b8/b7b85cb830ded99cd50bf47b7907d7da903193a1a1a3da7bfa11c2bd442b26b7.jpg)  
  

![](https://i.ormos.uk/c0/fd/c0fd58e4802ff294b0f741e4efb19b6b7d99323e846ba1e529674745a551172b.jpg)

**Name**: Ali Aintrazi  
  
**Age**: 24  
  
**Hometown**: Matthews, NC  
  
**Number of GP/PT Top 8s?**? Zero, but hopefully not for long!  
  
**Record by format:** Standard: 6-0-2, Draft: 5-1  
  
**What Standard deck did you play, and why?** UB. It felt like it was well positioned in the metagame, and it has no real bad matchups.  
  
**What colors did you draft?** The first was UW Fliers and I went 2-1. The second was GW Overrun and I 3-0’d.  
  
**How important is it to be on the National team with Worlds in the United States this year?** It would be awesome to represent the U.S. It’s not as big for me as winning a PT, but grand nonetheless.  
  
**What do you think the biggest impact of the recent changes to Standard was?** It allows other decks to have a chance instead of CawBlade mirrors all the time. I’m glad it happened, and I hope we don’t have another powerhouse like Jace, the Mind Sculptor, or Stoneforge Mystic in Standard.  
  
**What do you think the most powerful archetype is in *M12* draft?** White/X. White just has so many good commons. It’s the best color for this draft.  
  

![](https://i.ormos.uk/d6/c0/d6c0dc7f27878afbcb7116862bfde846c983f9df7baf976d31691aca519152c4.jpg)

**Name**: James McLeod  
  
**Age**: 19  
  
**Hometown**: Fuquay-Varina, NC  
  
**Number of GP/PT Top 8s?**? 0  
  
**Record by format:** Standard: 6-2, Draft: 5-1  
  
**What Standard deck did you play, and why?** Pyromancer Ascension. I felt it had good matchups on most of the field.  
  
**What colors did you draft?** UB aggro with two Mind Controls then RB Bloodthirst.  
  
**How important is it to be on the National team with Worlds in the United States this year?** Pretty important. The U.S. has to win that team trophy this year!  
  
**What do you think the biggest impact of the recent changes to Standard was?** Forcing people to add the "picking a deck for the metagame" skill to their toolbox instead of just grinding CawBlade mirror matches.  
  
**What do you think the most powerful archetype is in *M12* draft?** Probably any deck with Mind Control. Other decks are competitive, but not on the same level.  
  

![](https://i.ormos.uk/aa/64/aa6464ca56b30a911abe6d0abe6d23939a65098da5a22f1e06abaa2e09d6a147.jpg)

**Name**: Haibing Hu  
  
**Age**: 30  
  
**Hometown**: Houston, TX  
  
**Number of GP/PT Top 8s?**? 0  
  
**Record by format:** Standard:6-2, Draft: 6-0  
  
**What Standard deck did you play, and why?** CawBlade. I was given the deck by my friend David Thomas, who got an updated list from Gerry Thompson in the last minute. Gerry is a really good player.  
  
**What colors did you draft?** BR and UB. I draft a lot on **Magic** Online and sometimes post my draftcaps on texasmagiczone.com under phuqdao.  
  
**How important is it to be on the National team with Worlds in the United States this year?** Kind of important. Maybe a 7 on a scale of 1 to 10.  
  
**What do you think the biggest impact of the recent changes to Standard was?** I don’t really play much Standard, so I can’t give an in-depth answer. If you look at the results, the changes made very little difference.  
  
**What do you think the most powerful archetype is in *M12* draft?** I have no idea. Personally, I just like to draft whatever is open. Getting ate picks is a lot more important than being in an archetype. Although Wizards is getting better at designing synergistic Limited formats overall, core sets are still core sets.  
  

![](https://i.ormos.uk/4a/b3/4ab355d2b4ef94616cd11ae5dcba5cd483f53dd8a801179ae85818de9928710a.jpg)

**Name**: Owen Turtenwald  
  
**Age**: 22  
  
**Hometown**: West Allis  
  
**Number of GP/PT Top 8s?**? 8 GP +8  
  
**Record by format:** Standard: 6-2, Draft: 5-1  
  
**What Standard deck did you play, and why?** CawBlade, duh.  
  
**What colors did you draft?** UR and GB.  
  
**How important is it to be on the National team with Worlds in the United States this year?** It would be a pretty huge honor to represent the U.S. at Worlds. I want to be on the team really bad, more than I can describe. It’s really cool.  
  
**What do you think the biggest impact of the recent changes to Standard was?** There is less CawBlade in the field now and more free wins.  
  
**What do you think the most powerful archetype is in *M12* draft?** GB with 5 Greater Basilisks  
  

![](https://i.ormos.uk/7b/8b/7b8bfe03230aa55056d16350a11c9beb049fd081594fa86e018b724476e7acc6.jpg)

**Name**: David Ochoa  
  
**Age**: 30  
  
**Hometown**: Hayward, CA  
  
**Number of GP/PT Top 8s?**? 2 GP / 0 PT  
  
**Record by format:** Standard: 6-1-1 Draft: 6-0  
  
**What Standard deck did you play, and why?** CawBlade. It rewards good play and is quite resilient.  
  
**What colors did you draft?** UB and RB.  
  
**How important is it to be on the National team with Worlds in the United States this year?** More important than normal because defending the home turf is a big game.  
  
**What do you think the biggest impact of the recent changes to Standard was?** Creature decks became more viable.  
  
**What do you think the most powerful archetype is in *M12* draft?** White-based fliers. White is the deepest and has a lot of fliers. With cards like Stormfront Pegasus, Griffin Riders, and Griffins, white-based flier decks can open with brutally fast draws.  
  

![](https://i.ormos.uk/68/ef/68efb76aee6b2c5056b4accc82aca7ca7726e9625cfa0300eae52db0cecac349.jpg)

**Name**: Noah T Koessel  
  
**Age**: 18  
  
**Hometown**: Eureka, CA  
  
**Number of GP/PT Top 8s?**? 0  
  
**Record by format:** Standard: 7-1, Draft: 2-1, 2-1  
  
**What Standard deck did you play, and why?** CawBlade, because Alex Bertoncini convinced me to play it and the Holla-twins were playing it.  
  
**What colors did you draft?** RB ad UR.  
  
**How important is it to be on the National team with Worlds in the United States this year?** I think it’s pretty important because you get to represent your entire country against the rest of the world.  
  
**What do you think the biggest impact of the recent changes to Standard was?** The fact that many decks were no longer obsolete and so much worse than CawBlade.  
  
**What do you think the most powerful archetype is in *M12* draft?** Probably RB Bloodthirst if you get the nuts or a deck with a lot of Griffins and Griffin Riders.  
  

![](https://i.ormos.uk/27/8b/278b5fd45f7632d85f36b2fbe4b87dfe178ee13c8a4ef74ae6379ede0180efcf.jpg)

**Name**: Brandon Nelson  
  
**Age**: 26  
  
**Hometown**: St. Paul, MN  
  
**Number of GP/PT Top 8s?**? 0  
  
**Record by format:** Standard: 6-1-1 Draft: 5-1  
  
**What Standard deck did you play, and why?** UW CawBlade. It’s the nuts!  
  
**What colors did you draft?** GR and GU  
  
**How important is it to be on the National team with Worlds in the United States this year?** It’s very important to me. I’ve always wanted to rep the U.S. with the best in the world! USA! USA! USA!  
  
**What do you think the biggest impact of the recent changes to Standard was?** Banning Stoneforge Mystic was right, but not Jace, the Mind Sculptor!  
  
**What do you think the most powerful archetype is in *M12* draft?** BR Bloodthirst. It’s very aggressive and it’s hard to stop.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/14/e0/14e016eb9ef2e960d1cb417847b967370464025a3e46fa1df4c80a701e55e782.jpg)

**Name**: Luis Scott-Vargas  
  
**Age**: 28  
  
**Hometown**: Oakland, CA  
  
**Number of GP/PT Top 8s?**? 7 GP, 4 PT  
  
**Record by format:** Standard: 7-0-1 Draft: 4-2  
  
**What Standard deck did you play, and why?** CawBlade, because it’s still really good, though much worse than before.  
  
**What colors did you draft?** WR and BUG (I had to keep it fair to each color)  
  
**How important is it to be on the National team with Worlds in the United States this year?** It’s awesome regardless, and with Worlds ten minutes from my house, I’d especially like to be on the team!  
  
**What do you think the biggest impact of the recent changes to Standard was?** CawBlade is now a beatable deck, and people can actually pick from a variety of good decks.  
  
**What do you think the most powerful archetype is in *M12* draft?** Bloodthirst Aggro.

## U.S Nationals Quarterfinals – David Ochoa (CawBlade) vs. Noah Koessel (CawBlade)

*by Marc Calderaro* 

![](https://i.ormos.uk/b1/77/b1773b0314fd2945a9dbf4aa45188257a1dea8866f0b01df97bd02cc68f08a3f.jpg)

### Game One

Noah Koessel had to mulligan to five. Boo. This allowed Ochoa to get a clear advantage with [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Squadron+Hawk) s. Early on, [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Squadron%2BHawk)s abounded, as per usual in this type of mirror match-up, as Koessel, though down in cards, used his own [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Squadron%2BHawk)s to refill his grip. And, again as usual without equipment on either side, [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Squadron+Hawk) s soon abounded their way to the graveyard. Ochoa had the upper hand in the early game, netting some nice damage in with flyers, but Koessel soon even back up the board at zero. Though at a severely reduced life total; it was 15-8. He seemed poised fairly well, considering his card disadvantage.

Koessel was able to resolve a [Spellskite](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spellskite) , though that just powered up Ochoa's [Timely Reinforcements](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Timely+Reinforcements) to some sort of usefulness and Ochoa cast a de-flyered [Spectral Procession](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spectral+Procession) . He ticked his opponent down to 7, and upon seeing a freshly cast [Consecrated Sphinx](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Consecrated+Sphinx) on the other side of the board, killed everything with a [Day of Judgment](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Day+of+Judgment) .

Koessel still had the man-land in [Celestial Colonnade](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Colonnade) , so after thinking long and hard about the activation, he sent in the flyer against an open [Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Island) and [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge) .

"Cards?" Ochoa asked.

"Four." Ochoa rifled through the graveyard, then [Dismember](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dismember) ed the attacker. 11-7.

On Ochoa's next turn, he tried for a [Gideon Jura](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gideon+Jura) that was immediately [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) ed to join the litany of dead [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Squadron%2BHawk)s, cast [Preordain](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Preordain) s, and cracked Fetchlands all sitting together in the graveyard. Jura's demise was ok; Ochoa resolved the [Jace, the Mind Sculptor](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%2C+the+Mind+Sculptor) the next turn. He left open the [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge) mana necessary to bin another [Celestial Colonnade](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Colonnade) when it fool-heartedly tried to attack.

Another Gideon came down and it was two Planeswalkers to zero. Not the fairest of numbers, one must admit. And when Ochoa's own U/W flying land came in, Koessel had to bring out his bag of tricks: [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil) the Jura, block the big flyer with [Inkmoth Nexus](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Inkmoth+Nexus) , then [Dismember](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dismember) . He was down to 3 life, with a [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) and a friendless [Sword of Feast and Famine](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Sword%2Bof%2BFeast%2Band%2BFamine) left in his hand to Ochoa's grip of six.

Yet another Colonnade from Koessel allowed him to take out Jace and we were back to square one. Well, until Ochoa cast a [Consecrated Sphinx](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Consecrated+Sphinx) . Then we were back to a different kind of square one. It just took another [Dismember](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dismember) on the Colonnade blocker to cause Koessel to scoop them up and shuffle for the next game.

**David Ochoa 1 – 0 Noah Koessel**

![](https://i.ormos.uk/a3/b6/a3b6bde3e195dd894bced9517f56e4df735c36d21cfe52e7fd52fc401e51ac78.jpg)*David Ochoa*

### Game Two

Koessel got to keep all seven cards, and this time it was Ochoa who reshuffled his deck to try his hand again; he stayed at six. Both players spent the early game [Ponder](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ponder) ing to make their hands a little more prepared for the battles to come.

[Emeria Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Emeria+Angel) , [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Squadron+Hawk) and [Sword of Feast and Famine](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sword+of+Feast+and+Famine) were Ochoa's gas in-hand. On Turn 4, into four untapped mana, Ochoa resolved a [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Squadron+Hawk) to negate his early mulligan. Koessel responded by simply showing the [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge) and Ochoa moved his Colonnade to the yard, though Koessel's next play of [Jace Beleren](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace+Beleren) was [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) ed away.

The two players traded blows with [Day of Judgment](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Day+of+Judgment) for [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Squadron%2BHawk)s and [Oblivion Ring](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Oblivion+Ring) for [Sword of Feast and Famine](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Sword%2Bof%2BFeast%2Band%2BFamine)s. Koessel showed some sort of advantage with another [Jace Beleren](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace+Beleren) and a couple [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Squadron%2BHawk)s, but the life totals were still 19-18, hardly a large margin either way. Ochoa had an [Inkmoth Nexus](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Inkmoth+Nexus) to keep the Jace from doing too much, and a [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) for the [Consecrated Sphinx](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Consecrated+Sphinx) to keep it from doing, well, anything. So they both sat, each at seven cards, jostling for position.

Finally, when Ochoa tapped out, Koessel was able to resolve and equip a [Sword of Feast and Famine](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sword+of+Feast+and+Famine) onto one of his two [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Squadron+Hawk) s. Ochoa chumped with his own lonely [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Squadron%2BHawk), and replied with [Emeria Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Emeria+Angel) and a land drop. He passed with just enough mana to activate [Inkmoth Nexus](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Inkmoth+Nexus) or cast [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) . Koessel called his counterspell bluff and cast a [Gideon Jura](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gideon+Jura) , having it jump to eight counters upon entering the field.

Koessel was now up 11-18, and had two more cards in his hand. Ochoa made two free blockers with an [Arid Mesa](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arid+Mesa) , then laid a Gideon of his own into two untapped mana, with three on his side to pay for soft counters. Koessel allowed the Planswalkers to trade, then allowed his other blue Planeswalker to depart when his [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil) on the attacking Angel was [Spell Pierce](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Pierce) d.

More Angels and Sphinxes were inefficient, as another Wrath sent them all packing without much ado. Koessel saw Ochoa was short on cards, so a fresh [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Squadron%2BHawk) did wonders when equipped with something that causes discard. [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge) cleared the way of any manlands, and Koessel took his opponent to 7, then 4. and soon, nothing.

**David Ochoa 1 – 1 Noah Koessel**

![](https://i.ormos.uk/42/69/42693b4751386150c9b6d0c9fb2f02d94d0c5e56ce9c27a384d66c973cdffbe6.jpg)*Noah Koessler*

### Game Three

Koessel went to Paris once, and stayed at six. So far each game was won by the player who hadn't mulliganned. Would this be the same?

The early turns saw Ochoa get a leg up with a [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Squadron+Hawk) and a [Sword of Feast and Famine](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Sword%2Bof%2BFeast%2Band%2BFamine)s to just Koessel's [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Squadron%2BHawk), and a [Spell Pierce](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Pierce) d [Oblivion Ring](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Oblivion+Ring) solidified his position further.

Ochoa got in one hit, and Koessel's hand was just [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Squadron%2BHawk)s and a [Deprive](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deprive) . He had three of the 1/1 flyers in play and a tapped [Celestial Colonnade](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Colonnade) when he passed back to Ochoa, who added insult to injury with another sword equipped to another [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Squadron%2BHawk). Koessel had no choice up to chump it up. The next turn, Ochoa made it 18-10.

His next draw was blank. "I'm at ten?" Koessel double-checked.

"Yeah."

Koessel scooped up the cards. He had a decent hand, but multiple [Spell Pierce](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Pierce) s played spoiler rather well.

**David Ochoa 2 – 1 Noah Koessel**l

![](https://i.ormos.uk/3c/f3/3cf3a0e49138180ff0100013aa68d986031a7af19dae3f72acf814bf742a67ca.jpg)

### Game Four

The mulligan-win-predictor was not active this game as both players kept seven cards for the first time in the match-up.

Dueling [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Squadron%2BHawk)s (surprising, I know) started for both combatants, as an early Jace from Koessel met a [Spell Pierce](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Pierce) . Ochoa took the early life lead of 20-13 with the help of Koessel's [Dismember](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dismember) on an [Emeria Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Emeria+Angel) , but a [Sword of Feast and Famine](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Sword%2Bof%2BFeast%2Band%2BFamine) equipment threatened to even the field. Ochoa cast a second Angel, dropped a fetchland, then passed the turn back to Koessel. He gladly wiped the board; the totals after a land-crack was 18-11.

The two refilled quickly with [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Squadron%2BHawk)s (surprising, I know), but where Koessel had an equipment, Ochoa had a [Consecrated Sphinx](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Consecrated+Sphinx) . Koessel equipped and swung in and Ochoa sent one of his [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Squadron%2BHawk)s packing. They were momentarily even on cards, but the Sphinx threatened to change that in a heartbeat. As [Gideon Jura](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gideon+Jura) came down through a [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) , Ochoa changed more than just handsizes, taking down the equipped [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Squadron%2BHawk).

Koessel was sitting on [Dismember](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dismember) , [Revoke Existence](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Revoke+Existence) , [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Squadron+Hawk) and another [Sword of Feast and Famine](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sword+of+Feast+and+Famine) in his hand. He went into the tank, full of thought, and ripped a Gideon off the top. This prompted more tank time. Ochoa was tapped out, so there were no shenanigans to be had. Koessel deemed it best to tap out and trade Planeswalkers.

A [Ratchet Bomb](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ratchet+Bomb) from Ochoa, who then [Spell Pierce](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Pierce) d Koessel's four-life [Dismember](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dismember) (17-3). Koessel was in block-Sphinx mode, and Ochoa was in draw-infinity-cards mode.

It only took another turn for Koessel to extend his hand and send David Ochoa to the semifinals.

**David Ochoa 3 – 1 Noah Koessel**

Congrats to David Ochoa!

## U.S Nationals Quarterfinals – Brandon Nelson vs. Luis Scott-Vargas

*by Tim Willoughby* 

Here at the quarterfinals of the U.S. National Championships Brandon Nelson sat down ready to square off against Luis Scott-Vargas. In some respects this best of five match would be the most important of all the matches for the players in the top 8. The winner would at least become the alternate National Team member, while the loser would have to content themselves with the prize and Pro Points, but no Worlds invite. That Luis Scott-Vargas already has an invite did not dampen his desire to win in the slightest. For Brandon Nelson, a win would qualify the Midwest grinder for Worlds, and put him in a nice spot Pro Points-wise to be able to make a run on the latter portions of the season.

### Game One

It would be Brandon Nelson going first in this match, thanks to his position within the Swiss part of the event.

Neither player had a mulligan, and while Brandon started on a series of dual-lands, Luis was content with a Beta [Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Island) and a Beta [Plains](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plains) . Each player was cagey about running much out there too soon – resolving that first [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Squadron+Hawk) can be quite important if you are ever to form a flock. A [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge) from Brandon took out [Celestial Colonnade](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Colonnade) from Luis, and soon after there was more [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge) action, as each player went after more man-lands.

The first real spell of the game, barring a [Preordain](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Preordain) or two, was a [Sword of Feast and Famine](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sword+of+Feast+and+Famine) from Luis, who thanks to [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge) activations was down to just three basic lands. It was hit by a [Spell Pierce](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Pierce) , and this gave Brandon the opportunity to get a [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Squadron+Hawk) on the board. A second [Sword of Feast and Famine](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sword+of+Feast+and+Famine) did stick for Luis, but he could only look on as Brandon cast a [Gideon Jura](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gideon+Jura) , and immediately used its +2 ability.

Luis, slightly on the back foot due to his mana issues, cast a [Timely Reinforcements](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Timely+Reinforcements) , for 6 life and three tokens (represented by the rather spiffy 'Ancestral LSV' tokens that ChannelFireball debuted at Gencon Indianapolis 2011. Brandon fired right back with one of his own, and thanks to another [Gideon Jura](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gideon+Jura) +2, he was able to force many of the tokens to trade off.

Both players had more than the 20 life they had started with, and it was Brandon who had threats on the board to go about changing that. Luis used a [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) to stop extra [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Squadron+Hawk) woes, but had no answer to Nelson's own [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) when it stopped [Emeria Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Emeria+Angel) . Brandon looked to press his advantage, casting a [Jace Beleren](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace+Beleren) to draw a card (with the -1 abililty) and then attacking with his team, including Gideon, to put Luis to 13. Still more [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge) s from Brandon set Luis back down to three lands, and left the ChannelFireball pro in rough shape to get back in the game.

In a desperation move, Luis used two copies of [Dismember](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dismember) in an attempt to off Gideon. They were successful, but attacks left LSV on just two life. He'd need a [Day of Judgment](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Day+of+Judgment) to stay in the game, and when it didn't come, he packed in his cards.

**Brandon Nelson 1 – 0 Luis Scott-Vargas**

![](https://i.ormos.uk/6d/bb/6dbbbee84e2237c2960ca50e536119ecf00faf4bf645fcae84c927621fd40e88.jpg)

### Game Two

Sat where I was, I was able to see all of Luis' sideboarding for this match. Here's what he did:  
 OUT 3 [Dismember](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dismember) , 2 [Timely Reinforcements](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Timely+Reinforcements)  
 IN 2 [Mental Misstep](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mental+Misstep) , 2 [Azure Mage](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Azure+Mage) , 1 [Jace Beleren](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace+Beleren)

Game Two began much as game 1 had, without early plays from either competitor, barring [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge) activations to take down multi-lands. Luis got a few swings in with [Inkmoth Nexus](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Inkmoth+Nexus) , but was content to hold back from casting actual spells. He played out a second Nexus, and in spite of the potential for [Celestial Colonnade](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Colonnade) 's ability to animate and block, he was happy to attack with one. Brandon didn't bite, going to three poison.

For Nelson's turn, he played an [Inkmoth Nexus](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Inkmoth+Nexus) of his own. Would this end LSV's poisonous onslaught? Luis did have a different plan, casting [Gideon Jura](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gideon+Jura) as his first spell of the game, with three lands up to cast spells to protect it. The planeswalker resolved, and immediately gained a couple of loyalty.

The answer from Nelson was not a counterspell, but an [Oblivion Ring](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Oblivion+Ring) on his turn, which resolved without incident. Each player now had so many lands that the bulk of the countermagic that either might present would be hard pressed to achieve much. Nelson resolved a [Sword of Feast and Famine](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sword+of+Feast+and+Famine) . Luis' response was to fire back with [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil) on that [Oblivion Ring](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Oblivion+Ring) , and to follow up with an [Emeria Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Emeria+Angel) . It took two copies of [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) to stop the angel. Two more that Luis would not need to worry about.

[Oblivion Ring](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Oblivion+Ring) came back to deal with Gideon, the one non-land permanent that LSV controlled. [Inkmoth Nexus](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Inkmoth+Nexus) then got animated and equipped to deal 3 poison to LSV, and force him to discard a Day of Judgment. Brandon passed confidently with all his lands untapped. The only play from Luis was a tapped [Celestial Colonnade](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Colonnade) , and when Nelson cast [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Squadron+Hawk) , it was able to fetch three more copies without incident.

Luis went for the haymaker; [Consecrated Sphinx](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Consecrated+Sphinx) . With three mana up and five more cards left in hand, he seemed confident in his play, which Brandon only had one [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) for. What Brandon also had though was an [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil) with kicker. No cards for Luis just yet.

Sensing a good spot to get stuck in, Brandon animated his [Celestial Colonnade](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Colonnade) , and attacked with it and an equipped [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Squadron+Hawk) . The first real damage of the game, it put LSV to 12, from the 19 he'd been at thanks to an [Arid Mesa](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arid+Mesa) early in the game.

As quickly as Brandon filled the board with copies of [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Squadron+Hawk) , Luis cleared it with a Day of Judgement. The fourth hawk came along, and got equipped. There was enough mana left over, that Nelson could even cast [Deprive](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deprive) on [Consecrated Sphinx](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Consecrated+Sphinx) when LSV tried again to get the big flyer into play.

Brandon swung with an equipped [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Squadron+Hawk) , and [Inkmoth Nexus](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Inkmoth+Nexus) , putting LSV to 9 life and 4 poison. A second [Consecrated Sphinx](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Consecrated+Sphinx) got hit by another [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil) , keeping Luis very much on the back foot. More attacks put LSV at 6 life and 5 poison. A post-combat [Gideon Jura](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gideon+Jura) from Nelson looked as though it could lock the whole game up. Luis finally got to resolve his Sphinx, and draw some more cards, but on low life it was unclear if he'd have the luxury of time to get back in the game even if he had the cards to do it.

A [Dismember](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dismember) took out the [Inkmoth Nexus](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Inkmoth+Nexus) blocker that LSV had, leaving him at 3 life and 7 poison after attacks. Scott-Vargas drew his card for the turn, and conceded.

**Brandon Nelson 2 – 0 Luis Scott-Vargas**

![](https://i.ormos.uk/07/71/0771414674fcebef5c8b759fac530854505437357963d61c260feb5dfe334038.jpg)*Luis Scott-Vargas*

### Game Three

If this were a match anywhere outside the top 8 of this competition, it would all be over. However, in this top 8, with best of five matches, there was still the potential for Luis to get right back in the match, by rattling off three straight wins. He would be on the play again for game 3, ready to start his fight back.

LSV had a lot of copies of [Celestial Colonnade](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Colonnade) to begin game three, and an early [Sword of Feast and Famine](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sword+of+Feast+and+Famine) , which resolved. Next up was an [Emeria Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Emeria+Angel) , which met a a [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) . Luis had perhaps been a little greedy in casting the angel before playing his Beta [Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Island) , as it left him without the mana required to Leak back. That said, it seemed likely that the game would go on for long enough that he would get other targets for it, and had the Angel resolved, immediately getting value from it could have been a deciding factor in the game.

Luis was aggressive in throwing out questions to Nelson, having a [Jace Beleren](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace+Beleren) next, whose -1 ability drew LSV a card. The following turn it drew both players a card, before another [Emeria Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Emeria+Angel) came down, this time resolving without incident. It died to a [Dismember](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dismember) , but not before giving Luis a bird token.

A [Timely Reinforcements](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Timely+Reinforcements) from Nelson brought his life total back up again, and allowed Nelson to get a bit of board presence. None of those soldiers could block an equipped bird token though, forcing Nelson to discard a [Preordain](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Preordain) , and allowing LSV to untap his lands. He slammed down a second [Sword of Feast and Famine](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sword+of+Feast+and+Famine) , which got hit by [Deprive](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deprive) , not that Scott-Vargas really seemed to mind.

Nelson kept Jace in check a little with attacks, while Luis went for it with an [Azure Mage](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Azure+Mage) . In combination with a [Sword of Feast and Famine](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sword+of+Feast+and+Famine) attacker, this had the potential to allow LSV to draw well ahead in the race to draw a lot of cards, and forced Brandon to chump block a 3/3 bird with an [Inkmoth Nexus](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Inkmoth+Nexus) . He tried for [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil) on the sword, to finish off that bird token, but was thwarted by a [Spell Pierce](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Pierce) .

Nelson finally killed Jace off, but was faced with different ways in which Luis could draw cards. An [Oblivion Ring](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Oblivion+Ring) took out [Sword of Feast and Famine](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sword+of+Feast+and+Famine) , and at end of turn, LSV drew his first extra card with [Azure Mage](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Azure+Mage) . A [Gideon Jura](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gideon+Jura) with +2 looked set to lock up the red zone for Brandon for a while, and Nelson did everything he could to swiftly deal with the planeswalker, animating an [Inkmoth Nexus](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Inkmoth+Nexus) and casting a [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Squadron+Hawk) to add to his team. Luis simply kept using the +2 ability, and passed with lands up to draw more cards.

Nelson's attacks only got Gideon to 1 loyalty, as LSV was willing to throw out an [Inkmoth Nexus](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Inkmoth+Nexus) to block. At end of turn he used [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil) with kicker on [Oblivion Ring](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Oblivion+Ring) , to get back his sword, and drew a card with [Azure Mage](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Azure+Mage) . Now was the time to get aggressive. Luis equipped his [Azure Mage](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Azure+Mage) , cast a few copies of [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Squadron+Hawk) , and got stuck in. He had just enough mana up to [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) an attempted [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil) on [Azure Mage](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Azure+Mage) , meaning that his attacks connected, and he could cast [Jace Beleren](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace+Beleren) , draw a card with him, +2 Gideon, and pass with five mana up. Brandon was at 15 still, but did not seem excited about the position he was in.

Luis traded off [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Squadron+Hawk) s with most of Nelson's board, and simply nodded as [Oblivion Ring](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Oblivion+Ring) again took out [Sword of Feast and Famine](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sword+of+Feast+and+Famine) . He had [Jace Beleren](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace+Beleren) , [Azure Mage](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Azure+Mage) , and soon made it a trifecta of card drawing options with a [Consecrated Sphinx](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Consecrated+Sphinx) . Two out of three of these died to a Day of Judgment, after which Nelson tapped out for [Sword of Feast and Famine](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sword+of+Feast+and+Famine) .

Luis' deck was looking quite thin at this point, but this did not stop him from casting [Preordain](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Preordain) . He resolved another [Conundrum Sphinx](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Conundrum+Sphinx) to dig further, before sending Gideon to attack, taking Nelson to 9 life. While Brandon did have a [Consecrated Sphinx](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Consecrated+Sphinx) of his own, he'd tapped down low enough to cast it that he couldn't stop an [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil) clearing the way for a lethal attack.

**Brandon Nelson 2 – 1 Luis Scott-Vargas**

### Game Three

Both players kept for game four, and again neither player launched into casting spells until they had quite a bit of land in play. [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge) from Luis took out a [Celestial Colonnade](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Colonnade) , but in terms of honest to goodness spells, there was just a turn 3 [Preordain](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Preordain) from Luis before a turn 7 [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Squadron+Hawk) from Scott Vargas, with three lands up. Once one hawk landed, it seemed to Nelson a good time to deploy one of his own.

A [Jace Beleren](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace+Beleren) from LSV met a [Spell Pierce](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Pierce) , which LSV could pay for while still having mana up to cast [Preordain](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Preordain) . Tapped out, Luis traded hawks with Nelson when Nelson went for Jace, and looked on as another [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Squadron%2BHawk) entered the battlefield for his opponent. He had two of his own, ready to protect Jace.

[Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Squadron%2BHawk)s gradually traded off, and the additional card drawing from LSV started to kick in, as he was able to hit all his land drops in a way that his opponent was not. Given the way that counterspells function in this matchup, with most being conditional counters, that extra mana would prove very valuable. A [Sword of Feast and Famine](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sword+of+Feast+and+Famine) from Luis let him be a little more aggressive, though Nelson did have the [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil) to keep things honest for a while.

Jace finally succumbed, but by this point LSV had [Azure Mage](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Azure+Mage) to keep his grip full. With 7 lands to his opponent's four, he had the luxury of options. [Azure Mage](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Azure+Mage) fell to a [Dismember](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dismember) , but Luis still had plenty of action. He equipped his hawk and got stuck in. An [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil) on the sword meant that Nelson only took 1, going to 15.

Nelson played a land and passed, looking on as [Gideon Jura](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gideon+Jura) with a +2 joined LSV's side of the team. LSV had the [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) ready when an end of turn [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil) with kicker tried to bounce the planeswalker, and a [Spell Pierce](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Pierce) for the [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) that Nelson held.

Now that LSV was tapped out, Nelson took the time to land a [Consecrated Sphinx](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Consecrated+Sphinx) . However, in upkeep LSV had an [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil) to stop Nelson drawing any cards. The Sphinx that LSV landed both drew him cards, and was enough to put the game away attacking alongside Gideon.

**Brandon Nelson 2 – 2 Luis Scott Varga**s

![](https://i.ormos.uk/27/17/27176241226d2b4dbe54fba5a5952da5744bdc628a7314fd8cc1a906dfd338e7.jpg)*Brandon Nelson*

### Game Five

In more normal circumstances, each of these 2-0 runs would have been a match. Here at U.S. nationals, with a best of five top eight, there would be one more game, to decide it all.

"Maybe we'll see a turn 2 [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Squadron%2BHawk) for the first time this match" joked LSV, of the fact that most games the 'Caw' noises didn't break out until turn 5 or later. He seemed buoyed by having clawed back from an 0-2 start. Just one game to go.

For that deciding game, Luis was on a mulligan, and looked on at a turn one (!) [Preordain](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Preordain) from Nelson. There was the turn two hawk as well, which LSV could match. The beats would be a little faster this game.

The first hawks traded, but there were plenty more to come. What there weren't more of for LSV though were lands, as he missed his third land drop.

[Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Squadron%2BHawk)ward.

With Brandon getting up to five lands in play before LSV had found his third, it was a despondent face that Luis presented as watched [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Squadron%2BHawk)s and a [Jace Beleren](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace+Beleren) come down for Nelson. He eventually got an [Inkmoth Nexus](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Inkmoth+Nexus) as a third land, but by this time there was a [Gideon Jura](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gideon+Jura) staring him down.

An [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil) bounced Gideon, after a small counterspell scuffle where a [Spell Pierce](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Pierce) from Nelson was stopped by [Mental Misstep](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mental+Misstep) . As Nelson continued to lay lands, and recast Gideon, Scott-Vargas' leg began to jiggle. He was in deep, and he knew it. An [Azure Mage](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Azure+Mage) from Luis was killed off by [Dismember](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dismember) , and Nelson started applying the beats with both Gideon and two [Inkmoth Nexus](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Inkmoth+Nexus) . Luis used this opportunity to send two Nexus of his own at Jace, but took a huge hit as Nelson played a sword, equipped to Gideon and swung.

Nelson had a [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) in hand, and that was enough to stop any last defence that LSV might have. Luis, gracious in defeat, extended his hand.

**Brandon Nelson defeats Luis Scott-Vargas 3-2, advancing to the semi-finals.**

## U.S. Nationals Quarterfinals – James McLeod vs. Ali Aintrazi

*by Nate Price* 

"I'm really sad you guys got paired up together in the first round," I told the players as they shuffled up for the first game.

"Us, too," Aintrazi admitted.

The judge asked if they were friends, and Aintrazi responded, "Yeah. That and we're the only non-CawBlade decks in the Top 8."

### Game One

Aintrazi mulliganned to six on the play. McLeod broke the ice with a [See Beyond](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=See+Beyond) on the second turn, but Aintrazi stopped him with a [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) . He then untapped and added "Baby" [Jace Beleren](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace+Beleren) to his side, immediately drawing himself a card. McLeod used a [Preordain](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Preordain) on his turn to sift some, searching for a [Pyromancer Ascension](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyromancer+Ascension) , as well as getting a copy of a useful spell into his graveyard.

Aintrazi cast [Inquisition of Kozilek](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Inquisition+of+Kozilek) on McLeod, seeing a hand of two [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil) s, [Burst Lightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Burst+Lightning) , [Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Island) , and [Misty Rainforest](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Misty+Rainforest) . He took the [Burst Lightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Burst+Lightning) , not wanting to put a copy of a card that McLeod had in his hand in the graveyard, and passed the turn. During the end of turn, McLeod used one of his two [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil) s to bounce Jace with kicker. That put one of his two copies in his graveyard, enabling the other to put a counter on his Ascension should he cast it. He then drew his card and passed the turn.

Aintrazi replayed [Jace Beleren](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace+Beleren) , attacked with a [Creeping Tar Pit](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Creeping+Tar+Pit) , and passed the turn right back. Continuing the trend of paying more spells on Aintrazi's turn than his own, McLeod aimed a [Burst Lightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Burst+Lightning) at Jace during the end of turn. Thanks to the copy that had been Inquisitioned earlier, he got the first counter on his Ascension. He drew his card and played a second copy of [Pyromancer Ascension](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyromancer+Ascension) , adding a safety net. Aintrazi kept animating and attacking, dropping McLeod to two over the next two turns.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/a4/19/a419df380e0f0e68d3b3366ab8e3d2f3662ff8dbcbfaeaf59a1f7b613b3262c4.jpg)

At the end of Aintrazi's turn, and interesting exchange took place. McLeod went to cast [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil) with kicker on his counterless [Pyromancer Ascension](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyromancer+Ascension) . Aintrazi responded with an [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil) of his own on the Ascension with a counter. McLeod sacrificed his [Misty Rainforest](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Misty+Rainforest) to fetch an [Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Island) enabling him to [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) . Unfortnately, as the spells resolved in order, he went so far as to resolve his original [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil) without putting a counter on his Ascension, and Aintrazi stopped him when he tried to afterwards. The Ascension triggers upon *casting* of the spell, not resolution, so with the spell resolved, the time to add the counter had passed. Undeterred by his mental hiccup, McLeod untapped, added the second Ascension to his side, and then cast a [Preordain](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Preordain) to turn one of his Ascensions on. He followed that with a second copy, which he got to copy thanks to his Ascensions, and then a third, turning them both on. After that, he got to double up on a [Gitaxian Probe](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gitaxian+Probe) , drawing a trio of cards.

From that point winning was elementary. Aintrazi got to attack him down to three with [Creeping Tar Pit](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Creeping+Tar+Pit) s, but couldn't actually finish him. With his hand now full of cards, McLeod aimed a [Burst Lightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Burst+Lightning) and a pair (really sextet) of [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) s at Aintrazi to finish him off.

**James McLeod 1 – Ali Aintrazi 0**

### Game Two

McLeod started the second game rummaging through his deck and building a graveyard. He managed to get a single [Ponder](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ponder) off before Aintrazi [Duress](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Duress) ed him, taking [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) from a hand with [Gitaxian Probe](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gitaxian+Probe) , a pair of [See Beyond](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=See+Beyond) s, [Preordain](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Preordain) , [Halimar Depths](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Halimar+Depths) , and an [Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Island) . McLeod returned the favor, spying on Aintrazi with a [Gitaxian Probe](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gitaxian+Probe) , revealing [Jace Beleren](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace+Beleren) , [Grave Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grave+Titan) , [Mystifying Maze](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystifying+Maze) , [Darkslick Shores](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Darkslick+Shores) , and some [Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Island) s.

"Nice Maze."

"I know. I'm going to get your Sphinx! Woo!"

McLeod used his [Preordain](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Preordain) and a [See Beyond](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=See+Beyond) to give him one of more or less all the cards that matter in his graveyard. Now, all he had to do was draw a [Pyromancer Ascension](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyromancer+Ascension) . Aintrazi got his [Jace Beleren](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace+Beleren) down and started drawing cards with him. McLeod decided to use his second [See Beyond](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=See+Beyond) to dig a little deeper into his deck, finally finding his first [Mountain](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mountain) . That enabled him to play a [Pyromancer Ascension](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyromancer+Ascension) , setting him up to start building up. Aintrazi used the final loyalty of his [Jace Beleren](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace+Beleren) to draw a card before replacing it and drawing a second off his fresh Planeswalker. He passed the turn with [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) (or [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil) ) mana available. A [Gitaxian Probe](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gitaxian+Probe) ensured McLeod he was safe, revealing that a [Consecrated Sphinx](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Consecrated+Sphinx) and a bunch of lands had joined the [Grave Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grave+Titan) in Aintrazi's hand. He had nothing.

McLeod started small, using another Probe to draw some cards. He passed the turn with four mana available, choosing to use it to cast a pair of [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil) s with kicker to kill the Zombie Tokens made by Aintrazi's [Grave Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grave+Titan) immediately after they came down. He then untapped and started drawing an obscene number of cards. [See Beyond](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=See+Beyond) copied and filled up his hand. [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) doubled up to kill the Titan. [Ponder](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ponder) twinned and filled up his hand. Now in complete control thanks to Aintrazi's poor draw, McLeod passed the turn with a full hand and mana for a [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) available. He got to use it when Aintrazi tried to cast the [Consecrated Sphinx](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Consecrated+Sphinx) he had drawn earlier, getting to untap with a ton of lands in play.

Not feeling comfortable enough to simply go for things with his Ascension, he decided to play a [Consecrated Sphinx](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Consecrated+Sphinx) instead, passing the turn with one red open. Aintrazi merely animated his [Creeping Tar Pit](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Creeping+Tar+Pit) and attacked. McLeod used a [Burst Lightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Burst+Lightning) to hit the Tar Pit and Aintrazi, but Aintrazi used [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) to save his attacker. McLeod dropped to 9. He untapped and sent his Sphinx at Aintrazi, after which he set up a second [Pyromancer Ascension](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyromancer+Ascension) before turning his burn loose on Aintrazi to finish him off, just in case he had a [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) . Aintrazi had nothing but lands, and died a fiery death.

**James McLeod 2 – Ali Aintrazi 0**

![](https://i.ormos.uk/35/77/3577b26b313a47a30ff2d1b98134987ac868cc83cb2d970a65f64e397275636b.jpg)*James McLeod*

### Game Three

McLeod mulliganed to six, prompting Aintrazi to remark, "First mulligan of the match."

"You mulliganed game 1," McLeod reminded him.

"Oh yeah. *Man*, that game was so *close*," Aintrazi sighed.

Aintrazi played a first-turn [Creeping Tar Pit](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Creeping+Tar+Pit) , which caused McLeod to ask, "Where are your first-turn Inquisitions?"

He found out in short order when Aintrazi aimed a copy of the spell at him on the second turn. McLeod paid mana for a [Mental Misstep](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mental+Misstep) to stop it, but Aintrazi had a [Darkslick Shores](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Darkslick+Shores) and a second Inquisition to strip a [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) from a hand with a [Scalding Tarn](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scalding+Tarn) , [See Beyond](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=See+Beyond) , [Burst Lightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Burst+Lightning) , and [Gitaxian Probe](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gitaxian+Probe) . McLeod drew his card and played a [See Beyond](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=See+Beyond) . When Aintrazi used [Duress](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Duress) to strip him of [Gitaxian Probe](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gitaxian+Probe) on the following turn (no free cantrips into lands for you!), he revealed two new cards: a [Consecrated Sphinx](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Consecrated+Sphinx) and an [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil) .

For his turn, McLeod drew into a [Ponder](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ponder) , using it to snag himself a third land, leaving him with [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil) , Sphinx, and [Burst Lightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Burst+Lightning) in hand. Aintrazi started to assume control, adding to his resources with a [Solemn Simulacrum](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Solemn+Simulacrum) , who immediately started to attack on the following turn. A [Jace Beleren](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace+Beleren) followed, and when Aintrazi went to use him to draw a card, McLeod used [Burst Lightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Burst+Lightning) to kill the Planeswalker, limiting Aintrazi to that one card. McLeod drew a land for his turn, played it, and passed the turn.

Since that flurry of discard, Aintrazi had been firmly in the driver's seat. He animated his Tar Pit and sent it alongside the Simulacrum, dropping McLeod to 12. During the next attack, he used [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil) with kicker to try and return the Simulacrum, dropping to 11 from a [Scalding Tarn](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scalding+Tarn) . Aintrazi had the [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) to stop the mana-light McLeod, and the attack dropped him to 6. McLeod made a [Pyromancer Ascension](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyromancer+Ascension) on his turn, passing things back to Aintrazi. Aintrazi activated a pair of his [Creeping Tar Pit](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Creeping+Tar+Pit) s and sent all his creatures over, prompting the concession from McLeod.

**James McLeod 2 – Ali Aintrazi 1**

### Game Four

McLeod mulliganned again in the fourth game of the match. When Aintrazi tried to hit him with [Inquisition of Kozilek](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Inquisition+of+Kozilek) on his first turn, McLeod was ready with a [Mental Misstep](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mental+Misstep) . This time, the torrent of discard spells stopped right there, with Aintrazi making a [Jace Beleren](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace+Beleren) instead, using his +2 ability to keep him safe from McLeod's burn.
