Nelson and Ochoa spent the first six turns of the game casting and trading [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Squadron+Hawk) s, until there were none left in either player's deck.

Nelson's was a bit light on colored mana, as his first five lands were an [Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Island) , a [Seachrome Coast](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Seachrome+Coast) , a couple of [Inkmoth Nexus](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Inkmoth+Nexus) es, and a [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge) (which he used to destroy Ochoa's [Celestial Colonnade](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Colonnade) ). Nelson's [Jace Beleren](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace+Beleren) got [Spell Pierce](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Pierce) d, and with only a [Inkmoth Nexus](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Inkmoth+Nexus) untapped, Ochoa was able to resolve a [Gideon Jura](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gideon+Jura) without any fear of counterspells.

Nelson (who was holding a [Gideon Jura](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gideon+Jura) , that he was clearly itching to cast as it would legend rule away Ochoa's [Gideon Jura](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gideon+Jura) and get the two players back to more even footing) didn't find a fifth land on his turn, and instead had to use two [Dismember](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dismember) s to attempt to kill Ochoa's Gideon. Ochoa was ready with a [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) , and – even though Ochoa had the mana to pay for it – Nelson immediately used a [Spell Pierce](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Pierce) to get his opponent to tap low (giving himself a better chance of resolving his [Gideon Jura](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gideon+Jura) if he drew another white source on his next turn).

Ochoa's played a post-combat [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge) , and immediately used it to take out Nelson's [Seachrome Coast](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Seachrome+Coast) , leaving Nelson with only an [Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Island) and two [Inkmoth Nexus](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Inkmoth+Nexus) es for mana.

A few attacks, and an [Emeria Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Emeria+Angel) later, and Ochoa was on to the Finals!

**Final Result: David Ochoa 3 – Brandon Nelson 1**

## U.S. Nationals: Third Place Playoff – Haibing Hu (CawBlade) vs. Brandon Nelson (CawBlade)

*by Marc Calderaro* 

So this match-up is a big deal. I mean, sure there's a difference between the winner of Nationals and the Runner-Up – every time there's a card disagreement when planning for Worlds, the winner can just say, "I forgot, which one of us is the National Champion again? Oh, it's *not* you? I thought not." But between the third member on the team and the alternate is a different story. And that's not even mentioning that Worlds is on America's turf this year, so there's even more riding on the line.

Brandon Nelson knows about line-riding, coming so close to the train multiple times. This weekend marks the *second* time he had to make the Finals to get enough points for the next Pro level, and this is the *second* time he's finished Top 4. The saving grace is that he's completely psyched to represent Team USA. The Minnesota native has rocked this convention hall all weekend and he just needs to rock it a little more.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/20/43/204379571ee592922bee1922a1706efb6350f272859aa38c1907b2d9201ac71d.jpg)

Here to crush his pitiful optimistic Rocky-like dreams is Texas' Haibing Hu. A long-time circuit player, Hu made his big-stage debut all the way back in Pro Tour—Nice in 2002. He has a fantastic story from that trip about German customs officers and emergency passports shipped from America. Ask him sometime. Luckily, since Worlds in on the home front, there should be less customs troubles this time around. Hu, in his Top 8 profile declared on a scale of 1 to 10, representing America was "maybe a 7." A 7?! For that, Hu, I'm openly rooting for Nelson. Whatcha going to do about it, America-hater? Haibing Hu? More like Haibing Boo!

The two shuffled up for the first game.

### Game One

Hu won the die roll, and added to his card disadvantage by going down to six. He thought hard about his one-land hand before electing to keep. Brandon Nelson's less-suspect grip was [Preordain](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Preordain) , [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) , [Timely Reinforcements](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Timely+Reinforcements) , [Consecrated Sphinx](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Consecrated+Sphinx) , and three land. He kept in short order.

Like a totally boss, Hu ripped an [Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Island) off the top to play a [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Squadron+Hawk) and act like it was never an issue. While, Nelson, for whom it actually never was an issue, passed the turn right back. Hu, failing to continue his boss-like land-drawing activities, spent the next few turns attacking for one, then discarding – first a [Timely Reinforcements](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Timely+Reinforcements) , then a [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Squadron+Hawk) . Speaking of the Reinforcements, Nelson made the totals 20-24 when he cast his own, backed by a [Spell Pierce](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Pierce) to answer Hu's.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/d9/69/d9697e0334fc56823c465bafd851a730069abd38ab018bde4da6fc9aa4dd89f3.jpg)

Nelson sat on a Sphinx and three [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) , while Hu had three of his own, though with an [Oblivion Ring](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Oblivion+Ring) , [Day of Judgment](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Day+of+Judgment) and another Hawk instead of the Sphinx. Hu hit his fourth land and threw Hawks at Hawks. It was 16-23, and it looked like Hu had drawn out of his land troubles, though he still didn't have the excess to pay for [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) s the way Nelson had.

One [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) down for Nelson on a [Timely Reinforcements](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Timely+Reinforcements) , but he declined on Hu's [Day of Judgment](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Day+of+Judgment) to clear the board. Beware, Nelson! As Camus might say, "Don't wait for the Last Judgment; it happens every day." However, Nelson calmly followed up with a [Gideon Jura](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gideon+Jura) and paid for Hu's insuing [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) , so perhaps the Minnesota **Magic** player knows better than some old, dead French existentialist. Though the Planeswalker was promptly Ringed into Oblivion, Nelson paid nine mana for his Sphinx + [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) the next turn and began drawing many, many cards.

Haibing Hu used his last Leak, his last card, to make Brandon Nelson pay extra to cast and equip his [Sword of Feast and Famine](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sword+of+Feast+and+Famine) . But for this game, those three [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) s needed to be more effective than "Pay 3 more for your spells." Hu drew for his turn, saw Brandon was holding back six cards, and scooped up his own.

**Brandon Nelson 1 – 0 Haibing Hu**

I bet if we made Brandon score his excitement about Nationals on a 1-to-10 scale, he would say something like, "807," and stuff. You hear that Hu?! 807!

![](https://i.ormos.uk/77/ea/77ea2cb33c2deb36d889e42357ff017b1edf80ce8e75ca2b9c4573a0deefd2ca.jpg)

### Game Two

Nelson's keeper was [Revoke Existence](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Revoke+Existence) , [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Squadron+Hawk) , [Spell Pierce](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Pierce) , [Day of Judgment](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Day+of+Judgment) , [Timely Reinforcements](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Timely+Reinforcements) and land. Whereas Hu's was [Jace Beleren](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace+Beleren) , [Gideon Jura](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gideon+Jura) , [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) and double Hawk. They jockeyed for position – Nelson casting Hawk after Hawk, Hu drawing off Jace the Smaller and using some 1/1 fliers of his own – until the scores were 15-20 in Nelson's favor.

It was now Nelson who was land-light, and making him pay two more for his [Timely Reinforcements](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Timely+Reinforcements) with a [Spell Pierce](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Pierce) allowed Hu to comfortably cast his [Gideon Jura](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gideon+Jura) , make the Hawk and three 1/1s attack his new Planeswalker, and still leave two mana open. Nelson resolved a [Sword of Feast and Famine](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sword+of+Feast+and+Famine) but declined to equip it, leaving two mana up for Hu's turn. Hu finally gave Nelson a Revoke target in a Sword of his own. The two Pierced each other, then Hu equipped the sword to his only creature – the last [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Squadron+Hawk) not sitting in a graveyard. He made the totals 13-11 with a nine-point attack (Gideon hopped into the fray), cycled an [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil) on the opposing Sword, used [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge) on Nelson's, and passed the turn.

Nelson's soldiers took two loyalty counters off Gideon and the Revoke took down the Sword. The Minnesotan tried to [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) Hu's [Emeria Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Emeria+Angel) , but the last card in the Texan's hand was a Leak of his own. Nelson chuckled and the scores soon became 13-6.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/9a/b0/9ab0d8614b148932145e3e477c8d5d370acc86eab11a9ac4f60a08324f5e5faa.jpg)

Nelson still had the [Day of Judgment](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Day+of+Judgment) and finally used it with Hu in top-deck mode. Nelson's two-card grip had that Sword and an [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil) . His off-the-top [Gideon Jura](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gideon+Jura) was answered by Hu's off-the-top [Consecrated Sphinx](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Consecrated+Sphinx) . The big flyer drew two for Hu before going back to his hand with the Roil. Hu took 8 and it was 7-6.

Nelson continued his board presence with [Sun Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sun+Titan) returning [Jace Beleren](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace+Beleren) and drawing a card; Hu's Sphinx was appearing less and less awesome. Gideon, Titan and a [Celestial Colonnade](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Colonnade) attacked, and Hu was forced to concede.

**Brandon Nelson 2 – 0 Haibing Hu**

Ok, Hu's still evil or whatever, but I think my allegiance is changing. I like rooting for the underdog. So I've ditched my whole "America-yeah" thing, because isn't rooting for the guy who's down 0-2 even *more* American, especially when that guy's from Texas? If you think about, I'm sure you'll see I'm right. Go, Haibing! Texans unite!

### Game Three

They both kept and would you believe [Preordain](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Preordain) s and Hawks dominated the early game? Hu's three ruled the skies, because though Nelson [Preordain](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Preordain) ed twice, he was stuck without Land #4. He cast a defensive Hawk and passed with seven awesome cards in his hand, discarding yet another awesome card – [Consecrated Sphinx](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Consecrated+Sphinx) .

The two continued like this until the graveyard was full with just about every Hawk. Hu, keeping up the land-pressure, [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge) d his opponent as soon as he hit four mana and attacked until the scores were 13-20. After combat he cast an [Emeria Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Emeria+Angel) , and when it was [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) ed with Nelson's last two mana, he thought for a bit about how to respond. He cracked his two fetches and used his own Almost-a-Counterspell with [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) .

Nelson wasn't concerned. He tapped out for Gideon, forcing the Angel and Hawk to attack it, then on the following turn, made his Planewalker go down to two counters and assassinated the 3/3 token-maker. Hu made the scores 13 up when he tried to [Dismember](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dismember) a fresh Hawk, and though it was [Deprive](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deprive) d, he had the [Deprive](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deprive) of his own to make -5/-5 a reality. His Hawk and bird token took the last two counters off Gideon. Nelson cast his Sword then sunk to 11.

With a [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) , Revoke and Oblivion still left in his grip, he animated the [Celestial Colonnade](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Colonnade) , only to have it [Dismember](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dismember) ed. It was 9-9, and Hu attacked for two a turn with his flying 1/1s. [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil) and [Timely Reinforcements](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Timely+Reinforcements) were waiting in the wings. 7-9, 5-9, O-Ring removes Nelson's Jura and [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) saves it from [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil) , 3-9, 1-9….

Brandon Nelson drew off the top. It was the last turn he had to find an answer.

He didn't find it.

**Brandon Nelson 2 – 1 Haibing Hu**

See? Rootin' for the underdog. Oh yeah.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/f7/7f/f77f104563669ca4c986e3d634e6f5119015959d3ad2c7d8d0ab47a45666f4ef.jpg)

### Game Four

[Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge) , [Emeria Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Emeria+Angel) , [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) and land was good enough for Hu, but Nelson had higher aspirations, so he went down to six. He was happy to [Preordain](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Preordain) into two lands immediately after keeping a hand with only one, and the first four turns passed without significance.

Hu's first play was a [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Squadron+Hawk) , which was [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) ed, which was [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) ed, which was [Spell Pierce](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Pierce) d. The white creature went to the bin. His next play of [Emeria Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Emeria+Angel) at least made a token before having her limbs torn off by a [Dismember](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dismember) .

Hu eventually got his Hawk wish, and [Spell Pierce](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Pierce) d the kicked [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil) that tried to make his token disappear. Nelson trailed 13-19, but had [Timely Reinforcements](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Timely+Reinforcements) after Hu's Jace that evened the battlefield and the score. Hu then traded two Hawks and a bird token for three soldier tokens. Soon it was a Hawk and a Jace versus Nelson's shiny new Gideon with eight counters.

Brandon had five land so he would have to tap out to Swords up his [Gideon Jura](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gideon+Jura) . The remainder of his hand was a [Day of Judgment](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Day+of+Judgment) , a [Dismember](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dismember) and an [Oblivion Ring](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Oblivion+Ring) . He kept all five land open and attacked Jace with his 'walker. Hu [Dismember](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dismember) ed the attacker down to a 1/1 so it only brought the blue dude to two counters.

Hu had nine land, including two [Celestial Colonnade](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Colonnade) s, one of which attacked into the 7-loyalty Gideon. Nelson returned the [Dismember](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dismember) ing favor, but he was able to actually kill his target. It was 14-11 and Gideon took up the Sword and attacked Hu's face. The last Hawk gladly jumped to the rescue, as Hu's last three cards in hand were just more land. Thankfully, he drew a Gideon off the top to clear the other one, and his Jace was looking stronger than a creature-less Sword.

The extra cards drew him into a [Consecrated Sphinx](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Consecrated+Sphinx) and then *those* extra cards drew him into the [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) to protect it from [Day of Judgment](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Day+of+Judgment) , as Nelson still had only six land and was unable to pay the extra cost. Two Swords came down and Haibing Hu makes his opponent go to a last game. This game had been all about land draws. Well, land draws and some timely non-land ones.

**Brandon Nelson 2 – 2 Haibing Hu**

Ok, so now Hu is no longer the underdog, so my allegiance has switched yet again. This time, it's for America. Who ever wins this game, it's a win for America! USA! USA! USA!

![](https://i.ormos.uk/73/1a/731acec6f3fe5d2fbd2b54b84bd8dfeae99b1ed979ffa0a6437b8d2e39e9cfb5.jpg)

### Game Five

They both kept their openers to make for an honest rubber game. Hu's opening Colonnade left a nook for [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Squadron+Hawk) to fill for Nelson's second turn. And hey, what do you know, the next turns consist of land-drops, trading [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Squadron+Hawk) s, and making better draws with [Preordain](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Preordain) .

Counter battles and Hawks attacks made the score 16-15, with Nelson ahead in life but behind on board. He tried to at least get a hold of the battlefield with a [Timely Reinforcements](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Timely+Reinforcements) , but a kicked [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil) by Hu on his own Hawk, backed up by a [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) on a [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) denied any creature change.

Hu got no extra cards from his new [Consecrated Sphinx](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Consecrated+Sphinx) with an upkeep-step [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil) . Nelson tried to [Deprive](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deprive) the biggie on the way back down, but Hu had the [Spell Pierce](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Pierce) . Nelson had drawn into another [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil) , so he was able to delay the 4/5 while cycling through his deck, but he was getting frustrated. When his [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) tried to take out the Sphinx on its third time down, Hu's defensive leak made Brandon all-out mad. Did Hu draw every single counterspell?!

"Attack." Nelson sent in the 1/1 Flyer into the 4/5.

Hu chuckled. "No blocks." It was 13-13 when Nelson's [Day of Judgment](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Day+of+Judgment) cleared the board.

Nelson had two cards to Hu's six, and he was watching his 2-0 lead, and a spot on the Nationals team slipping away. His Hawk looked sickly compared to Hu's hawk with a big ol' Sword attached. It had been a long weekend, a long match, and a long game. The graveyards were almost as tall as the libraries, and there were still both above 10 life and the largest creature on the board was a base 1/1.

A [Gideon Jura](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gideon+Jura) resolved for Nelson, but was exiled with the Ring. Nelson drew, hung his head, and tapped down to one mana, attempting to resolve a [Consecrated Sphinx](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Consecrated+Sphinx) .

He held his breath.

It resolved. He picked himself up and drew two cards on his opponent's draw step. Hu activated one of his three [Celestial Colonnade](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Colonnade) s, tried to equip the Sword to it, and still had mana left over to [Spell Pierce](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Pierce) Nelson's [Dismember](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dismember) . Nelson sunk to 2, discarded his last card, and drew off the top, hanging his head again.

It was an [Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Island) . He laid it, representing enough to activate one of his two Colonnades, and passed back to Hu. Hu again activated the land, but equipped the Sword to the Hawk and swung with both. This is when a judge decision came that affected the entire match.

It was ruled that Hu had waited too long after Nelson placed his animated his Colonnade and Nexus in front of his attackers, to have any responses before blocks. The judge asked Hu to make it clear in the future when he was or was not prepared to enter the next step in combat. This meant that both his creatures were blocked when he [Dismember](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dismember) ed the Colonnade to save his Hawk. Neither player was sure of it, but this play changed the outcome of the match. Nelson drew cards, still at two life, and on Hu's next attack, animated his second Colonnade.

"Let me think about that," Hu clearly stated, and then he said, "Go ahead."

After blocking the Hawk, he tapped the manland to use his last [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge) to take out the attacking 4/4. Hu went to 5, using a [Dismember](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dismember) to return the Colonnade-killing favor.

2-5. Nelson had drawn so many extra cards from the Sphinx it was ridiculous, and facing down two Colonnades and the suited Hawk, and way too close for comfort, drew a Jace into an [Inkmoth Nexus](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Inkmoth+Nexus) . Hu passed without attacking; his two Colonnades were susceptible to the two [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge) s of Nelson. Nelson now had so many cards he was back to discarding. Before doing so he played a Swords and Hu's two Colonnades bit the dust. It was a Hawk with a Sword against a Sphinx with a Sword (and a Jace).

This game was getting ridiculous. Nelson had somehow, some way, clawed back into it and after drawing land after land with his extra Sphinx cards, he finally started drawing gobs of goodies. We'd entered the stage where both players constantly counted libraries and graveyards. Nelson had eleven cards left to draw. This would be the last turn of the match.

On Nelson's attack step, Hu cast [Divine Offering](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Divine+Offering) on the Swords, then took the damage. It was now 2-4. Nelson lost his possible blocker for the last Hawk when Hu sacrificed [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge) to kill [Inkmoth Nexus](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Inkmoth+Nexus) . Nelson had an [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil) , [Spell Pierce](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Pierce) , and [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) all sitting in his hand. He used the Nexus mana on an [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil) for the attacker. And when Hu [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) ed, Nelson counted his opponent's graveyard and considered his options for the last three mana – whether to tap out and save the other counters, or to use one and keep some land untapped.

He thought, re-counted graveyards, saw there were no more [Spell Pierce](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Pierce) s nor [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) s left in Hu's deck. Every single one of them had been spent. He counted again to be sure. Yup, three Pierces, four [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) s. Hu was all out of counters!

Nelson confidently tapped out to pay for the [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) .

He immediately regretted it.

Faster than a [Sudden Shock](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sudden+Shock) , Hu tapped two mana and slammed the one card Nelson forgot. The only card that could make his play the wrong one. Hu lifted his hand from the table, revealing to all the onlookers just what he was so excited about. A huge gasp fell across the room. It was [Deprive](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deprive) . The one-of [Deprive](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deprive) .

Nelson was in shock. After this whole weekend, how could it come down to forgetting about one measly sideboard card in a match-up he had started at 2-0? When he composed himself, he extended his hand and one of the most tense CawBlade mirrors ever played, finished in style.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/ea/df/eadfd13bd009b9a57ad9fa8d16d8c16d6c0c1c0d03d7b2ff33c60382bffd1c14.jpg)

**Haibing Hu 3 – 2 Brandon Nelson**

And we have a Texan on the U.S. Nationals team. Congratulations to both players! An outstanding match to cap off an outstanding weekend.

## U.S. National Championship Finals – Ali Aintrazi vs. David Ochoa

*by Brian David-Marshall* 

"You can only lose to CawBlade if they have [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Squadron+Hawk) on turn two, on the play," said one of Ali Aintrazi's pals as he waited around for the grueling mirror match between Ochoa and Nelson to play out. Ali nodded in agreement. He could not remember how many times he had played CawBlade on the weekend but he knew that the one game he dropped in the semifinals to it was the only game less he had taken against the archetype throughout the event.

For David Ochoa this had to fall under the category of just happy to be here -- not to say he would not like to win. He reached as far as the semifinals of last year's Nationals and then lost again in the playoff for the last slot on the team. This year he was the last member of the ChannelFireball squad in the Top 8 -- a lineup that included Luis Scott-Vargas and Owen Turtenwald.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/36/c1/36c19f7ff37707afdbebc8f59daf3d463f3e225fe58ac42cbca71a6c808bfa35.jpg)

### Game One

An [Inquisition of Kozilek](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Inquisition+of+Kozilek) from Aintrazi reveleaded a hand with [Dismember](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dismember) , [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) , [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Squadron+Hawk) , [Timely Reinforcements](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Timely+Reinforcements) , and two lands -- Ali took the Hawk.

Ochoa found a [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Squadron+Hawk) waiting on top of his deck and Ali slumped a little bit in his chair as his opponent announced: "I'll get the other two."

Aintrazi played [Jace Beleren](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace+Beleren) and drew a card. Ochoa attacked the Planeswalker down to one loyalty and played another Hawk. He had no fourth land to play the last one in his hand. Ali expended his Jace and played a fresh one. He made both players draw and pushed it to 5 loyalty with 2 Hawks out there.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/b1/50/b1504a08dbf8535395db967bc0584a1bd4c726b2e199b23acab242397d2b4a58.jpg)

Ochoa attacked Jace down to 3, played another Hawk and laid a Colonnade. Aintrazi played another planes walker -- [Liliana Vess](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Liliana+Vess) -- and plus-oned her to make Ochoa discard a [Timely Reinforcements](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Timely+Reinforcements) . Then he had them both draw a card. Ochoa attacked Liliana and played a Jace to nuke the Jace. Ali knew there was still a [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) in his opponent's hand and played his sixth land and upped Liliana. Ochoa discarded [Dismember](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dismember) . Then he offered up [Solemn Simulacrum](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Solemn+Simulacrum) ...

..."You are on how many cards?" asked Ochoa.

"I am on six."

Ochoa sighed and played [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) . Ali used his [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge) on the Colonnade to keep him off of killing the Planeswalker in one turn. Ochoa attacked Liliana down to 1, played [Emeria Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Emeria+Angel) and made a bird.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/9b/71/9b714ae1d7698c6a4a9accee0b31b9e64566cc52f03c21193d089a705909fd6b.jpg)

Liliana forced Ochoa to discard [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) . He held onto one card. Ali played Sphinx and during Ochoa's upkeep the ChannelFireball player bounced the Sphinx and drew safely. He attacked Liliana to death and used [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge) on Aintrazi's sixth land. Ali had another and replayed the Sphinx.

Ochoa attacked with five 1/1 fliers and the Sphinx ate one of them -- Ali went to 11. On Ochoa's next upkeep he killed the [Emeria Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Emeria+Angel) with [Dismember](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dismember) and took four in the air from the birds. Ali got perilously low from the birds swarming him each turn but he was racking up insane card advantage while the Sphinx lived turn after turn.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/16/86/1686c4162f2eb552389fbe7869db78349208a92481e86b0869a8a87fedfb8281.jpg)

Things looked grim when Ochoa stuck a [Gideon Jura](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gideon+Jura) but a timely [Karn Liberated](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Karn+Liberated) nuked the opposing planeswalker. Ali was at three life but was pulling away quickly. Ochoa kept on fighting but there was little he could do at a three card to one disadvantage every turn.

**Ali Aintrazi - 1 David Ochoa - 0** 

![](https://i.ormos.uk/f3/b1/f3b19a0dd9f8ed648d8976df6c7cc50a086261be97a2d665cb784139ed5ed491.jpg)

### Game Two

Ali went to five cards to start the game while Ochoa came out with a Hawk for two Hawks on turn two. Aintrazi's Kozilek's Inquisition saw the Hawks, [Azure Mage](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Azure+Mage) , Gideon, [Sword of Feast and Famine](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sword+of+Feast+and+Famine) , and two lands -- one which was a Nexus. He took the Sword. Ochoa swung in with his Hawk and played the [Azure Mage](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Azure+Mage) . [Doom Blade](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Doom+Blade) took out the two-drop while Ochoa continued to peck away with his Hawk. He played two more and said "go" but asked judge if it was too late to search. The judge said it was too late.

A [Despise](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Despise) from Ali revealed that the fourth Hawk was already in Ochoa's hand and Ali chided him -- "Sneaky" -- as he took Gideon. Ochoa played the fourth Hawk and over the next couple of turns the full air force managed to finish Ali off.

**Ali Aintrazi - 1 David Ochoa - 1**

![](https://i.ormos.uk/36/04/3604579a45d3e25da31909fb24a8f74d5fdd51258b8303ae8c7d6bf9b87eebab.jpg)

### Game Three

Ali played Inquisition and saw [Spell Pierce](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Pierce) , [Preordain](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Preordain) , [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil) , [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Squadron+Hawk) , [Emeria Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Emeria+Angel) , and two land -- as usual he took the Hawk. [Preordain](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Preordain) was crossed off Ali's little cheat sheet when Ochoa played the spell. Ali played his own [Preordain](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Preordain) to dig for cards -- there were 32 in the Top 8 so you will hear that quite a bit. To underscore the point, Ali played a second [Preordain](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Preordain) , laid a [Swamp](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Swamp) , and said go.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/07/d0/07d055da80202ede91753dadd452f436bfd3dbc1f6344b54a017b97a027665dc.jpg)

Ochoa played a fourth land and cast [Sword of Feast and Famine](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sword+of+Feast+and+Famine) -- with [Spell Pierce](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Pierce) in hand it was going to stick. The two players went draw-go for a couple of turns until Ali played [Solemn Simulacrum](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Solemn+Simulacrum) . It resolved but it was returned to hand with a kicked [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil) .

Ochoa played a sixth land and went to cast something and then thought better of it -- it seemed like he was considering [Consecrated Sphinx](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Consecrated+Sphinx) but wanted to leave [Spell Pierce](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Pierce) Mana up and finally played [Gideon Jura](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gideon+Jura) . Ali dug with [Preordain](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Preordain) and passed the turn. He was holding a pair of [Doom Blade](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Doom+Blade) and deployed them to kill a freshly cast [Emeria Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Emeria+Angel) when Ochoa went to equip it with his Sword. The other went to take out the animated Gideon.

Ali untapped to play [Consecrated Sphinx](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Consecrated+Sphinx) . Ochoa played a Sphinx of his own and defended it from [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) with his long held [Spell Pierce](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Pierce) . Ochoa did not want to get into a card drawing battle with Aintrazi and chose not to use his Sphinx -- while Ali drew the extras whenever he could.

Ochoa did get the Sword equipped to it and managed to get a hit in. He had a second Sword and equipped that as well. Ali played [Inquisition of Kozilek](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Inquisition+of+Kozilek) and drew out [Mental Misstep](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mental+Misstep) -- the last card in Ochoa's hand. He played [Karn Liberated](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Karn+Liberated) and exiled the Sphinx. It left Ochoa in a bad spot. He did not want to use his Jace, he could not animate and equip his Colonnade because he was a mana short, and he did not really went to leave cards laying around in his hand to get exiled by Karn.

Again the game dragged on for several more turns but once Ali's deck had that kind of advantage it was hard for the Caw deck to finish it off.

**Ali Aintrazi - 2 David Ochoa - 1**

### Game Four

After each player mulliganed Ochoa kicked things off with [Azure Mage](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Azure+Mage) . An Inquisition from Ali took Sword and [Despise](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Despise) took Gideon but then Ochoa drew Hawk the next turn.

Jace came down on turn three for Ali and each player drew a card. Ochoa attacked it and played land number four. Ali stuck with the booth players draw plan and played another [Despise](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Despise) to take another Gideon. When Ochoa drew a card with the Mage, Ali [Doom Blade](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Doom+Blade) d the 2/1.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/56/df/56dfc2d06f11ee32e72c2dbcf2269ff56fd874d9530e7a8e4363a8540201fdc9.jpg)

Ochoa played both Hawks and a Colonnade while Ali sent his Jace up to 5 loyalty. Ochoa dialed it back down to 2 and Ali bought some time by kicking an [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil) on a Hawk at the end of turn. He landed [Grave Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grave+Titan) and they both drew a card with Jace. Ochoa played a kicked Roil on the [Grave Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grave+Titan) at end of turn.

Ochoa played [Sword of Feast and Famine](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sword+of+Feast+and+Famine) and equipped a Hawk, played another Hawk, and attacked Ali who discarded a superfluous Jace. Ochoa untapped his lands and moved the Sword to his fresh Hawk before playing his last copy. Inquisition from Ali revealed [Dismember](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dismember) and thee lands. He played land 7 and passed the turn after [Preordain](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Preordain) .

Ochoa animated Colonnade and attacked with everything. Ali used [Doom Blade](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Doom+Blade) on the land and took 6, discarding [Grave Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grave+Titan) . Another Inquisition said the coast was clear for [Black Sun's Zenith](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Black+Sun%27s+Zenith) for three.

Ali played [Consecrated Sphinx](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Consecrated+Sphinx) and made a sound like Charlie Brown having the football yanked away at the last minute when Ochoa played a freshly drawn [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) . He played Karn the next turn and proceeded to take over the game with the planeswalker. Other cards Ali played over the remainder of the game included another Sphinx, [Volition Reins](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Volition+Reins) for the Sword, and [Liliana Vess](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Liliana+Vess) . The game was not close once Karn hit the table.

Congratulations to the new U.S. National Champion Ali Aintrazi and his two teammates David Ochoa and Haibing Hu!

![](https://i.ormos.uk/b8/c4/b8c4837354db75759bc397ef92e1f6b50b1d4cf67762d4758a5ac062853f554c.jpg)

## U.S. Nationals – Top Five Cards of the Weekend

*by Brian David-Marshall* 

You could have played Magic from the moment you got to Gen Con until the last minutes as they were breaking down the greatest four days in gaming. There were Block Championships, Vintage Championships, Legacy Championships, Grand Melee booster drafts, 8-man booster drafts -- and of course, the U.S. National Championship. There were a handful of cards that stood out from the rest of the hundreds and hundreds of different cards that saw play.

![Slash Panther](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Slash+Panther)

### Slash Panther

This is a card that is a decent in draft and has seen virtually zero play in Block, Standard, Legacy, and Extended tournaments and yet it is fast becoming a Vintage staple. You can power it out on turn one with a [Mishra's Workshop](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mishra%27s+Workshop) and a Mox. Add in a [Phyrexian Metamorph](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Phyrexian+Metamorph) and you have a turn three kill against an unwary opponent. It is also shockingly good at killing [Jace, the Mind Sculptor](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%2C+the+Mind+Sculptor) out of nowhere. In the Vintage Championships Ryan Glacklin took the deck as far as fourth place.

![Scavenging Ooze](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Scavenging+Ooze)

### Scavenging Ooze

Reed Hartman took a single copy of this Commander card -- a never-legal-in-Standard addition to the four [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) s he already played. According to the Legacy Championships finalist the card may have actually been better than the first four [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf) s when all was said and done.

![Goblin Fireslinger](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Goblin+Fireslinger)

### Goblin Fireslinger

What would you take out of your *Magic 2012* booster pack if you were drafting for the National championship; [Flameblast Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flameblast+Dragon) or [Goblin Fireslinger](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Fireslinger) ? According to Christian Valenti -- who like you at home thought "dragon" -- he should have taken the [Fireslinger](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fireslinger) . The card broke out this weekend as one of the staples of the aggressive goblin draft archetype that carried multiple people through their draft pods without a loss. Valenti managed to pull that pod out despite being "stuck" with a dragon.

![Azure Mage](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Azure+Mage)

### Azure Mage

Everyone likes card drawing -- you need only look at the [Consecrated Sphinx](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Consecrated+Sphinx) on either side of the finals -- but it was the ChannelFirebal crew that put three players into the Top 8 of Nationals with the CawBlade mirror breaking [Azure Mage](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Azure+Mage) in their sideboard. The perfect card to straddle control and beat down , you can expect to see CawBlade players everywhere following suit in in the coming weeks.

![Karn Liberated](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Karn+Liberated)

### Karn Liberated

US National Champion Ali Aintrazi showed everyone who the new Planeswalker on the block is when he used the "vindicate" ability on Karn to take care of pesky lands, chump blocking [Squadron Hawk](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Squadron+Hawk) s, and a [Consecrated Sphinx](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Consecrated+Sphinx) . He could have even started a new game in the last game but decided that he would rather just win instead with his army of Planeswalkers that included Karn, [Liliana Vess](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Liliana+Vess) , and [Jace Beleren](https://web.archive.org/web/20160504013952/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace+Beleren) .
